Distance and similarity measures on belief and plausibility under q-rung orthopair fuzzy sets with applications
Authors: Rashid Hussain, Zahid Hussain, Nadia M. Sarhan, Nizomiddin Juraev, Shams Ur Rahman
Published: 2024

ABSTRACT
Belief and plausibility functions based on evidence theory (ET) have been widely used in managing uncertainty. Various generalizations of ET to fuzzy sets (FSs) have been reported in the literature, but no generalization of ET to q-rung orthopair fuzzy sets (q-ROFSs) has been made yet. Therefore, this paper proposes a novel, simple, and intuitive approach to distance and similarity measures for q-ROFSs based on belief and plausibility functions within the framework of ET. This research addresses a significant research gap by introducing a comprehensive framework for handling uncertainty in q-ROFSs using ET. Furthermore, it acknowledges the limitations inherent in the current state of research, notably the absence of generalizations of ET to q-ROFSs and the challenges in extending belief and plausibility measures to certain aggregation operators and other generalizations including Hesitant fuzzy sets, Bipolar fuzzy sets, Fuzzy soft sets etc. Our contribution lies in the proposal of a novel approach to distance and similarity measures for q-ROFSs under ET, utilizing Orthopairian belief and plausibility intervals (OBPIs). We establish new similarity measures within the generalized ET framework and demonstrate the reasonability of our method through useful numerical examples. Additionally, we construct Orthopairian belief and plausibility GRA (OBP-GRA) for managing daily life complex issues, particularly in multicriteria decision-making scenarios. Numerical simulations and results confirm the usability and practical applicability of our proposed method in the framework of ET.

Introduction

There exist a lot of uncertain situations which are random, fuzzy, vague, ambiguous, unpredictable and imprecise. To handle such uncertain situations close to reality, Lotfi A. Zadeh1 proposed a theory named as fuzzy set (FS) in 1965, which is an extension of crisp sets. The term “fuzziness” describes the uncertainty in elements of a set. Zadeh1 was the first to propose FSs as an extended form of ordinary sets. In fuzzy set, we work on membership degree, and the non-membership degree is taken as (1-membership). However, in real-life scenarios, there are many situations where non-membership degree does not equal to (1-membership), so there is a need of hesitancy degree. To address this gap Atanassov2,3 was first to generalize the FSs to IFSs in 1986, with the consideration of membership degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu$$\end{document}μ, non-membership degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu$$\end{document}ν and degree of hesitancy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi$$\end{document}π with the constraint condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu + \nu + \pi = 1$$\end{document}μ+ν+π=1 or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu + \nu \le 1$$\end{document}0≤μ+ν≤1. The world didn’t stop with IFSs and researchers have modified the drawbacks in IFSs and later on, in 2013, Yager4,5 gave new concept which extends IFS to another concept, called PFSs which performs better than IFSs. The characterization of PFSs based on degrees of membership, non-membership and hesitancy, respectively with the condition that the sum of squares of all these three terms is equal to one4–10. In some sets, the IFSs can’t fulfill the required condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu + \nu \le 1$$\end{document}0≤μ+ν≤1, for example consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A = (0.5,\,\,0.6)$$\end{document}A=(0.5,0.6), then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le 0.6 + 0.5\not \le 1 = 1.1$$\end{document}0≤0.6+0.5≰1=1.1. Yager4,5 suggested the notion of Pythagorean fuzzy set (PFS) which handle the complex issues and fulfill the condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu^{2} + \nu^{2} \le 1$$\end{document}0≤μ2+ν2≤1. For instance, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A = (0.5,\,\,0.6)$$\end{document}A=(0.5,0.6), then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le 0.6^{2} + 0.5^{2} \le 1 = 0.61$$\end{document}0≤0.62+0.52≤1=0.61. A PFS can be termed mathematically as: let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda$$\end{document}λ be any PFS in G then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda = \left\langle {a,\mu_{\lambda }^{2} \left( a \right),\nu^{2}_{\lambda } \left( a \right),\pi^{2}_{\lambda } \left( a \right):a \in G} \right\rangle$$\end{document}λ=a,μλ2a,νλ2a,πλ2a:a∈G and satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu^{2} + \nu^{2} \le 1$$\end{document}0≤μ2+ν2≤1 where degree of uncertainty is given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi = \sqrt {1 - \mu^{2} - \nu^{2} }$$\end{document}π=1-μ2-ν2. Furthermore, there are still some cases where PFSs are unable to handle certain situations i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le 0.8^{2} + 0.7^{2} = 1.13\not \le 1$$\end{document}0≤0.82+0.72=1.13≰1. There was a need of further extension of PFSs. Therefore, in 2017, Yager11 initialized another candid extension by taking the qth power of membership and non-membership degrees, which he named as q-rung orthopair fuzzy sets. The characterization of q-ROFSs is same as PFS but they differ by constraint condition that is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu^{q} + \nu^{q} \le 1.$$\end{document}0≤μq+νq≤1. The uncertainty degree of q-ROFS will be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi = \sqrt[q]{{1 - \mu^{q} - \nu^{q} }}$$\end{document}π=1-μq-νqq. In other words, q-ROFSs are considered as the generalized forms of IFSs and PFSs. Reference12 gave q-rung orthopair fuzzy weighted averaging (q-ROFWA) operators and q-rung orthopair fuzzy weighted geometric (q-ROFWG) operators.

Literature review

Distance is being used as a tool to measure difference between two objects. First distance measure is proposed by Szmidt and Kacprzyk13. Szmidt and Kacprzyk13 proposed distance by using hesitance index. Wang and Xin14 had criticized on the studies of Szmidt and Kacprzyk13 that they were not much effective. Hamming distance15 and Euclidean distance16, and after that the Hausdorff distance was proposed by Grzegorzewski15 which was revised by Xu and Chen16. Yang and Hussain17 and Ali et al.18 also worked on distance and similarity measures of HFSs. Hussain et al.19 worked on distance measures on q-ROFSs. Many researchers worked on distance and similarities based on q-ROFSs such as11,12. Jawad20 proposed normed-based distance measures of q-ROFSs with applications. Ejegwa21 proposed new q-rung orthopair fuzzy distance-similarity operators with applications. Arora et al.22 suggested similarity measures for q-ROFSs and applications to decision making. Ziyue & Xiao23 proposed generalized Hellinger distance for multisource information fusion with applications. Wang et al.24 proposed novel distance measures of q-ROFSs with their applications.

Belief and plausibility is another emerging field to manage uncertainty based upon ET25–27 and they have been widely used in many areas. This framework improves comprehension, reasoning, and decision-making across various topics, ultimately resulting in more robust and well-informed judgments. It improves the understanding and assessment of unclear information. Dealing with uncertainty is crucial in several fields, including risk analysis, artificial intelligence, and decision-making. Belief and plausibility offer a systematic framework for reasoning and making well-informed decisions in the face of contradictory or inadequate data. Researchers incorporate subjective evaluations and evaluate the viability of alternative hypotheses in an effort to reflect the inherent uncertainty in real-world scenarios. Shafer’s theory26 infrastructure aims to generalize probability theory, which is challenging when addressing complex issues. Dempster25,26 initialize the theory of lower and upper probability. Soon after Shafer26 introduced belief and plausibility measures on usual subsets based upon Dempster theory. However, FSs and their extensions have been shown to be very practical and sensible tools for handling various forms of uncertainty that closely resemble real-world situations. Dymova and Sevastjanov28,29 interpreted IFS to ET. Hwang and Yang30 also proposed some belief and plausibility functions which were based on the work of Dymova and Sevastjanov28,29. Khalaj and Khalaj31–33 proposed similarity measures based on belief functions. Yang and Hussain34 proposed theory about belief and plausible measures on IFS by constructing belief and plausible TOPSIS. Hussain et al.35 interpreted belief and plausible measure of PFSs with application to BPI-VIKOR. Till now, no one has considered q-ROFSs in the context of belief and plausible functions. Therefore, we dedicate this study to investigating q-ROFSs in the frame work of belief and plausible functions, particularly focusing on their application in addressing complex problems of MCCDM. We suggest normalized belief and plausible functions on q-ROFSs which enable us to construct the OBPIs. This construction allows us to create new similarity measures between belief and plausible functions by defining Hausdorff metric on OBPIs. These similarity measures36 are then applied in recognition of different patterns and in MCCDM by using OBP-GRA.

Multicriteria decision making (MCDM) is a systematic approach to decision making, taking into account multiple factors and criteria to compare alternatives. By considering multiple perspectives, MCDM enables decision makers to conduct a comprehensive analysis of available options, identifying the most suitable choice. This approach is particularly valuable in situations where a single optimal solution doesn’t exist. Many researchers worked on MCDM37,38 and applied in different fields33,39–44. Jana and Hezam45 proposed multi-attribute group decision making method for sponge iron factory location selection problem. Hagag et al.46 used MCDM approach for machine selection. Some other researchers worked on MCDM such as24,47–50.

During the Covid-19 pandemic, vaccine selection became a complex issue. Although, numerous vaccines were available but selecting the most efficient one was challenging. As far as we know, no one has suggested a methodology to select best vaccines using q-ROFSs in the frame work of belief and plausible functions. Therefore, this research addresses the selection of the most efficient vaccine to combat the pandemic.

Research gap and limitations of existing literature

In the existing literature, no one has considered distance and similarity measures on belief and plausible functions under the context of q-ROFSs along with their applications. Also, the existing methods often fall short in effectively managing and analyzing multiple criteria under uncertainty. Additionally, no generalization of ET to q-ROFSs is reported so far.

Motivation

The motivation behind this research work is to fill crucial gaps in existing methodologies for handling uncertainty and imprecision in complex systems. Specifically, to develop novel distance and similarity measures for belief and plausible q-ROFS based on OBPIs. Traditional approaches in pattern recognition and clustering within the framework of Evidence Theory do not adequately address the complexities and uncertainties present in real-world data. By introducing these novel measures, we aim to significantly enhance the accuracy and reliability of these applications. Furthermore, our pioneering work in constructing OBP-GRA using the proposed distance measure targets the challenges in multicriteria decision-making processes. Existing methods often fall short in effectively managing and analyzing multiple criteria under uncertainty. Our method gives decision-makers a strong and complete tool that makes use of fuzzy logic's advantages to manage uncertainty and imprecision more effectively, and develop more effective tools for multicriteria decision-making where traditional methods have proven insufficient.

Our contribution

Our main contributions in this paper are as follow:

First, we develop novel distance and similarity measures for belief and plausible q-rung Orthopair fuzzy sets based on OBPIs which provide a more nuanced, appropriate and alternative way to measure fuzzy situations within q-ROFSs. Secondly, we apply these proposed similarity measures in pattern recognition and clustering, which is a pioneering effort that demonstrates the practical utility and effectiveness of our proposed measures in real-world applications. Finally, we construct OBP-GRA using the proposed distance measure to enhance the multi-criteria complex decision-making process, which offers a robust tool for decision-makers to evaluate and compare complex options more effectively.

Organization of paper

The remaining article is structured as follows:Section "Preliminaries" consists of fundamental concepts and definitions of q-ROFSs and ET.Section "Novel belief and plausible measures of q-ROFSs" includes the methodology for defining belief and plausible functions in terms of q-ROFSs along with some properties.Section "Distance measure of q-ROFS for belief and plausibility interval" is dedicated to creating OBPIs of q-ROFSs based on the suggested belief and plausible functions. We then utilize the concept of Hausdorff distance with OBPIs to construct novel distance and similarity measures for two q-ROFSs. Furthermore, some numerical examples are given in this section to demonstrate the reasonability and usefulness of our suggested measures.Section "Similarity measures of q-ROFSs for belief and plausibility intervals" includes the proposed similarity measures with their applications in pattern recognition and clustering.Section "Construction of Orthopairian belief and plausible GRA" describes the utilization of the GRA method to create OBP-GRA for managing daily life health related complex problem involving MCCDM.Section "Conclusions" includes our conclusion.

Preliminaries

We briefly describe ET and review fundamental definitions of IFSs, PFSs and q-ROFSs respectively. Then, we establish the connection between q-ROFSs and belief and plausibility functions. In a q-ROFS, the belief function is represented as membership function, and the plausibility function is represented as a non-membership function.

Dempster-Shafer’s evidence theory

The upper and lower probabilities were first coined by Dempster25 with the help of multi-valued mappings. A decade later, Shafer26 proposed the concept of belief and plausible measures of usual subsets based upon Dempster’s probabilities. Here, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m$$\end{document}m is called basic probability assignment (BPA), which allocates same weights to all subsets of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{M} \subseteq \tilde{\Theta }$$\end{document}M~⊆Θ~ from the existing evidences and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{M}$$\end{document}M~ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2^{{\tilde{\Theta }}}$$\end{document}2Θ~ when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m(\tilde{M}) \ne 0$$\end{document}m(M~)≠0 is known as focal element. Belief and plausibility measures are functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {Bl,Pl} \right\}:2^{{\tilde{\Theta }}} \to \left[ {0,1} \right]$$\end{document}Bl,Pl:2Θ~→0,1 written as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl(\tilde{M}) = 1 - Bl(\tilde{M}^{c} )$$\end{document}Pl(M~)=1-Bl(M~c) while \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl(\tilde{M}) = \sum {_{N \cap M \ne \phi } } m\left( {\tilde{N}} \right) = P^{ * } \left( {\tilde{M}} \right)$$\end{document}Pl(M~)=∑N∩M≠ϕmN~=P∗M~. Here, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl(\tilde{M}) = 1 - Pl(\tilde{M}^{c} )$$\end{document}Bl(M~)=1-Pl(M~c) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl(\tilde{M}) = 1 - Bl(\tilde{M}^{c} )$$\end{document}Pl(M~)=1-Bl(M~c).

Definition 1

Novel belief and plausible measures of q-ROFSs

First of all, q-ROFSs are interpreted under the contexts of ET and after the interpretation, the novel belief and plausibility functions on q-ROFSs are proposed. We use our proposed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Blz and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Plz functions to construct various similarities by using Hausdorff metric. In the framework of ET, whenever the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M is taken with complement \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^{c} ,$$\end{document}Mc, then the degree of belief will be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right) + Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}BlM+BlMc≤1. Therefore, due to this reason, there is a difference between ET theory and probability theory. Under the contexts of ET, the level of commitment to a proposition's complement and its degree of belief may not be the same. For total ignorance, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right) = Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right) = 0$$\end{document}BlM=BlMc=0, but from probabilistic view point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pro\left( M \right) = Pro\left( {M^{c} } \right) = 0.5$$\end{document}ProM=ProMc=0.5. So the belief function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right)$$\end{document}BlM unable to explain the belief committed to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M as it doesn’t explain doubt committed to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M that is the belief committed to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^{c} .$$\end{document}Mc. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Dou\left( M \right) = Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right)$$\end{document}DouM=BlMc is defined for the sake to completely describe the belief committed to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M. Salicone51–53 also discussed the doubt function.

Linkage between evidence theory and q-ROFSs

On a close observation, it is noticed that there is a linkage between ET and q-ROFS and they are closely relate with each other. As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu \left( z \right)$$\end{document}μz in q-ROFS is similar to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Blz in ET, so we can easily relate them and can be written as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( z \right) = \mu_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMz=μMz, which reflects that both degrees of membership and belief have a linkage and are interconnected by showing the same demonstration about certain happenings. In this scenario, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}μMz may be considered as the degree of belief of an event \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in M$$\end{document}z∈M, shown by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMz. The degree of non-membership that is one’s personal belief that the event \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in M^{c}$$\end{document}z∈Mc does not occur is denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{{M^{c} }} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMcz. Since, in q-ROFSs, membership and non-membership degree is expressed as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\langle {\mu \left( z \right),\nu \left( z \right)} \right\rangle$$\end{document}μz,νz and its complement is denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\langle {\nu \left( z \right),\mu \left( z \right)} \right\rangle$$\end{document}νz,μz. The complement of an event \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M in term of doubt’s degree is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{{M^{c} }} \left( z \right) = \nu_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMcz=νMz. i.e. it may be considered as the degree of doubt that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M does not happen. Indeed, the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMz and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{{M^{c} }} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMcz are personal judgments. So to completely describe the event \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M,$$\end{document}M, both belief function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMz and doubt function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Dou_{M} \left( z \right) = Bl_{{M^{c} }} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}DouMz=BlMcz should be better given. The doubt function is rarely used. In general, a complete description of event \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M is given by belief function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMz and plausibility function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}PlMz, and written as:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{M} \left( z \right) = 1 - Bl_{{M^{c} }} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}PlMz=1-BlMcz\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$= 1 - Dou_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}=1-DouMz\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$= 1 - \nu_{M} \left( z \right) = \mu_{M} \left( z \right) + \pi_{M} \left( z \right).$$\end{document}=1-νMz=μMz+πMz. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( {\mu \left( z \right),\nu \left( z \right),\pi \left( z \right)} \right)$$\end{document}μz,νz,πz of q-ROFSs have the condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu \left( z \right) + \nu \left( z \right) + \pi \left( z \right) = 1$$\end{document}μz+νz+πz=1, so it properly shows basic assignment function (BAF) under the context of ET. Therefore, we express ET in terms of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu \left( z \right)$$\end{document}μz and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu \left( z \right)$$\end{document}νz of q-ROFSs to demonstrate a newer definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Blz and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Plz function in a very simple and intuitive way.

Definition 4

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~ be a universal set and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M be a q-ROFS in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~, then the belief function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}BlMz will be,1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ Bl_{M} \left( z \right) = \mu_{M}^{q} \left( z \right)\;\;\forall \;\;z \in \tilde{Z} $$\end{document}BlMz=μMqz∀z∈Z~

Definition 5

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~ is a universal set and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M be a q-ROFS in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~, then the plausibility function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{M} \left( z \right)$$\end{document}PlMz will be,2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ Pl_{M} \left( z \right) = 1 - \nu_{M}^{q} \left( z \right)\,\,\,\,\forall \,\,\,\,\,z \in \tilde{Z} $$\end{document}PlMz=1-νMqz∀z∈Z~

Definition 6

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} Definition 7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} Definition 8

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Blz and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl\left( z \right)$$\end{document}Plz be the belief and plausibility functions which satisfy the following properties:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {P_{1} } \right):0 \le Bl\left( M \right) \le Pl\left( M \right) \le 1\,; $$\end{document}P1:0≤BlM≤PlM≤1;\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {P_{2} } \right):\,\,\,If\,M_{1} \subseteq M_{2} \to Bl\left( {M_{1} } \right) \le Bl\left( {M_{2} } \right)\,\,{\text{and}}\,\,Pl\left( {M_{1} } \right) \le Pl\left( {M_{2} } \right)\,; $$\end{document}P2:IfM1⊆M2→BlM1≤BlM2andPlM1≤PlM2;\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {P_{3} } \right):Bl\left( M \right) = 1 - Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right)\,\,{\text{and}}\,Pl\left( M \right) = 1 - Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right)\,; $$\end{document}P3:BlM=1-PlMcandPlM=1-BlMc;\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {P_{4} } \right):Bl\left( M \right) + Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right) \le 1\,\,\,{\text{and}}\,\,\,Pl\left( M \right) + Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right) \ge 1\,. $$\end{document}P4:BlM+BlMc≤1andPlM+PlMc≥1.

Theorem 1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~ be universal set and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M is subset of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~. Then, the proposed belief and plausible measures of q-ROFSs fulfill all the properties of Definition (8).

Property 1

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Bl\left( M \right) \le Pl\left( M \right) \le 1\,$$\end{document}0≤BlM≤PlM≤1.

Proof

For any q-ROFS it is known that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu_{{_{M} }}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) + \nu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) + \pi_{{_{M} }}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1,\forall \,z_{i} \in \tilde{Z},$$\end{document}0≤μMqzi+νMqzi+πMqzi≤1,∀zi∈Z~, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\,0 \le \mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1 - \nu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}0≤μMqzi≤1-νMqzi≤1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1,\,\,$$\end{document}0≤BlMzi≤PlMzi≤1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\,0 \le \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}0≤∑i=1nωiBlMzi≤∑i=1nωiPlMzi≤1. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Bl\left( M \right) \le Pl\left( M \right) \le 1$$\end{document}0≤BlM≤PlM≤1. Thus, property 1 is satisfied.

Property 2

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$If\,M_{1} \subseteq M_{2} \to Bl\left( {M_{1} } \right) \le Bl\left( {M_{2} } \right)\,\,{\text{and}}\,\,Pl\left( {M_{1} } \right) \le Pl\left( {M_{2} } \right)\,$$\end{document}IfM1⊆M2→BlM1≤BlM2andPlM1≤PlM2.

Proof

If we have two q-ROFS \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{1}$$\end{document}M1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{2}$$\end{document}M2 which follow the condition that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{1} \subseteq M_{2}$$\end{document}M1⊆M2 then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu_{{M_{1} }}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le \mu_{{M_{2} }}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right),$$\end{document}μM1qzi≤μM2qzi, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } \mu_{{M_{1} }}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } \mu_{{M_{2} }}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right),$$\end{document}∑i=1nωiμM1qzi≤∑i=1nωiμM2qzi, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\,Bl\left( {M_{1} } \right) \le Bl\left( {M_{2} } \right)$$\end{document}BlM1≤BlM2 and similarly we can prove \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl\left( {M_{1} } \right) \le Pl\left( {M_{2} } \right)$$\end{document}PlM1≤PlM2 by using condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{1} \subseteq M_{2}$$\end{document}M1⊆M2. So, property 2 is satisfied.

Property 3

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right) = 1 - Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right)\,\,{\text{and}}\,Pl\left( M \right) = 1 - Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right)\,$$\end{document}BlM=1-PlMcandPlM=1-BlMc.

Proof

By previous knowledge, we can say that, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right)\, = \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } \mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) = 1 - \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} + } \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } \mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) = 1 - \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} \left( {1 - \mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right)} = 1 - Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right)$$\end{document}BlM=∑i=1nωiμMqzi=1-∑i=1nωi+∑i=1nωiμMqzi=1-∑i=1nωi1-μMqzi=1-PlMc and similarly we can do it for the plausibility that is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl\left( M \right) = 1 - Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right)$$\end{document}PlM=1-BlMc. Thus, property 3 is satisfied.

Property 4

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right) + Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right) \le 1\,\,\,{\text{and}}\,\,\,Pl\left( M \right) + Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right) \ge 1\,$$\end{document}BlM+BlMc≤1andPlM+PlMc≥1.

Proof

By definition of both membership as well as non-membership property \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right)$$\end{document}0≤μMqzi and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\,0 \le \nu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}0≤νMqzi≤1. We can show it in the form of belief and plausibility measures that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}0≤BlMzi≤1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1,\,$$\end{document}0≤PlMzi≤1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right)$$\end{document}BlMzi≤PlMzi,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le 1 - Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right)\,,\,\,$$\end{document}BlMzi≤1-BlMc, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) = 1 - Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right)$$\end{document}PlMzi=1-BlMc, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) + Bl\left( {M^{c} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}BlMzi+BlMc≤1 and similarly for plausibility measure we can prove it as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) \le Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right),$$\end{document}BlMzi≤PlMzi, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 - Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right)\, \le Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right),\,$$\end{document}1-PlMc≤PlMzi, by combining these, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le Pl\left( M \right) + Pl\left( {M^{c} } \right)$$\end{document}1≤PlM+PlMc. Hence, all the properties are satisfied. □

Example 1

Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z} = \left\{ {z_{1} ,z_{2} ,z_{3} } \right\}\,$$\end{document}Z~=z1,z2,z3 be finite universes of discourse. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M be any q-ROFS in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M = \left\{ {\left\langle {z_{1} ,0.6,0.9} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{2} ,0.7,0.8} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{3} ,0.7,0.7} \right\rangle } \right\}.$$\end{document}M=z1,0.6,0.9,z2,0.7,0.8,z3,0.7,0.7. Assume that weights are assigned as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega_{1} = 0.6$$\end{document}ω1=0.6, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega_{2} = 0.25$$\end{document}ω2=0.25 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega_{3} = 0.15$$\end{document}ω3=0.15. Then from Eq. (1), the belief function will be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{1} } \right) = 0.216,\,\,\,$$\end{document}BlMz1=0.216, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{2} } \right) = 0.343,\,$$\end{document}BlMz2=0.343, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{M} \left( {z_{3} } \right) = 0.343$$\end{document}BlMz3=0.343 respectively. Likewise, the plausibility function will be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{M} \left( {z_{1} } \right) = 0.271,\,\,Pl_{M} \left( {z_{2} } \right) = 0.488,\,\,Pl_{M} \left( {z_{3} } \right) = 0.657$$\end{document}PlMz1=0.271,PlMz2=0.488,PlMz3=0.657. Hence, the belief and plausible measures from belief and plausibility function of Eqs. (3) and (4) will be:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ Bl\left( M \right)\, = \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } \mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right) = \left( {0.6} \right)\left( {0.216} \right) + \left( {0.25} \right)\left( {0.343} \right) + \left( {0.15} \right)\left( {0.343} \right) = 0.2668 $$\end{document}BlM=∑i=1nωiμMqzi=0.60.216+0.250.343+0.150.343=0.2668

Similarly plausibility function will be,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ Pl\left( M \right)\, = \sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\omega_{i} } (1 - v_{M}^{q} (z)) = \left( {0.6} \right)\left( {0.271} \right) + \left( {0.25} \right)\left( {0.488} \right) + \left( {0.15} \right)\left( {0.657} \right) = 0.3832 $$\end{document}PlM=∑i=1nωi(1-vMq(z))=0.60.271+0.250.488+0.150.657=0.3832

The comparison is clearly shown in Fig. 1.Figure 1Graphical comparison of belief and plausibility measures.

Graphical comparison of belief and plausibility measures.

Therefore, the above results fulfills the condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl\left( M \right)\, \le Pl\left( M \right)$$\end{document}BlM≤PlM.

Distance measure of q-ROFS for belief and plausibility interval

Here, orthoparian belief and plausibility interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M}$$\end{document}OBPIM of q-ROFSs are constructed using (1), (2). Hausdorff distance is then used to find the distance of orthoparian belief and plausibility intervals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M}$$\end{document}OBPIM and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{N}$$\end{document}OBPIN. Both the forward Hausdorff and backward Hausdorff distances are 5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \begin{gathered} \overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{H} \left( {M,N} \right) = \max_{{z_{i} \in M}} \left\{ {\min_{{z_{j} \in N}} \left( {\left\| {z_{i} - z_{j} } \right\|} \right)} \right\}, \hfill \\ \overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{H} \left( {M,N} \right) = \max_{{z_{j} \in N}} \left\{ {\min_{{z_{i} \in M}} \left( {\left\| {z_{i} - z_{j} } \right\|} \right)} \right\}. \hfill \\ \end{gathered} $$\end{document}H⌢M,N=maxzi∈Mminzj∈Nzi-zj,H⌢M,N=maxzj∈Nminzi∈Mzi-zj.

Hausdorff metric space is oriented and asymmetric, which means the forward distance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\left( {M,N} \right)$$\end{document}hM,N does not necessarily same as the backward distance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\left( {N,M} \right)$$\end{document}hN,M. Hausdorff metric is defined as:6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{H} \left( {M,N} \right) = \max \left\{ {\overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{h} \left( {M,N} \right),\overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{h} \left( {N,M} \right)} \right\}.\, $$\end{document}H⌢M,N=maxh⌢M,N,h⌢N,M.

For any two intervals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M = \left[ {m_{1} ,m_{2} } \right]\,$$\end{document}M=m1,m2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = \left[ {n_{1} ,n_{2} } \right]$$\end{document}N=n1,n2 the Hausdorff distance will be,7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{H} \left( {M,N} \right) = max\left\{ {\left| {m_{1} - m_{2} } \right|,\left| {n_{1} - n_{2} } \right|} \right\}. $$\end{document}H⌢M,N=maxm1-m2,n1-n2.

Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N$$\end{document}N be two q-ROFSs in the universes of discourse \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z} = \left\{ {z_{1} ,z_{2} ,...,z_{n} } \right\}\,$$\end{document}Z~=z1,z2,...,zn for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z_{i} \in \tilde{Z}$$\end{document}zi∈Z~, we define orthoparian belief-plausibility intervals of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z_{i}$$\end{document}zi in q-ROFS \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N$$\end{document}N for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i = 1,2,...,n$$\end{document}i=1,2,...,n as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ OBPI_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right) = \left[ {Bl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right),Pl_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right] = \left[ {\mu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right),1 - \nu_{M}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right], $$\end{document}OBPIMzi=BlMzi,PlMzi=μMqzi,1-νMqzi,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ OBPI_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right) = \left[ {Bl_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right),Pl_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right] = \left[ {\mu_{N}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right),1 - \nu_{N}^{q} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right]\,. $$\end{document}OBPINzi=BlNzi,PlNzi=μNqzi,1-νNqzi.

Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right)$$\end{document}OBPIMzi and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right)$$\end{document}OBPINzi are subintervals in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left[ {0,\,1} \right]$$\end{document}0,1. We then define the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M}$$\end{document}OBPIM of the q-ROFSs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N$$\end{document}N as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ OBPI_{M} = \left\{ {OBPI_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right):z_{i} \in \tilde{Z}} \right\}\,\,{\text{and}}\,\,OBPI_{N} = \left\{ {OBPI_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right):z_{i} \in \overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{Z} } \right\}. $$\end{document}OBPIM=OBPIMzi:zi∈Z~andOBPIN=OBPINzi:zi∈Z⌢.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{H} \left( {OBPI_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right),\,\,OBPI_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right)$$\end{document}H⌢OBPIMzi,OBPINzi is said to be Hausdorff distance between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right)\,\,$$\end{document}OBPIMzi and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right)$$\end{document}OBPINzi respectively. The Hausdorff distance between two orthoparian belief and plausible fuzzy intervals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M} \,$$\end{document}OBPIM and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{N} \,$$\end{document}OBPIN will be,8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ d_{BPH} \left( {OBPI_{M} ,\,OBPI_{N} } \right) = \frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\frown}$}}{H} \left( {OBPI_{M} \left( {z_{i} } \right),\,\,OBPI_{N} \left( {z_{i} } \right)} \right)} .\, $$\end{document}dBPHOBPIM,OBPIN=1n∑i=1nH⌢OBPIMzi,OBPINzi.

Now, the distance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d$$\end{document}d on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPIs$$\end{document}OBPIs is axiomatically defined below.

Definition 9

Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M}$$\end{document}OBPIM,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{N}$$\end{document}OBPIN and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{O}$$\end{document}OBPIO be the three \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPIs$$\end{document}OBPIs corresponding to three q-ROFSs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N$$\end{document}N and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\text{O}}$$\end{document}O on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z}$$\end{document}Z~. Then the distance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d$$\end{document}d of Eq. (8) on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPIs$$\end{document}OBPIs is said to be distance if it fulfills the below axioms:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {d_{1} } \right)\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,0 \le d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) \le 1; $$\end{document}d10≤dOBPIM,OBPIN≤1;\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {d_{2} } \right)\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0\,\,iff\,\,OBPI_{M} = OBPI_{N} ; $$\end{document}d2dOBPIM,OBPIN=0iffOBPIM=OBPIN;\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left( {d_{3} } \right)\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = d\left( {OBPI_{N} ,OBPI_{M} } \right); $$\end{document}d3dOBPIM,OBPIN=dOBPIN,OBPIM;

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( {d_{4} } \right)\,$$\end{document}d4 \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\,\,\,If\,\,OBPI_{M} \le OBPI_{N} \, \le \,OBPI_{O} \,$$\end{document}IfOBPIM≤OBPIN≤OBPIO be any three orthoparian belief-plausibility intervals, then, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) \le d\left( {OBPI_{N} ,OBPI_{O} } \right)$$\end{document}dOBPIM,OBPIN≤dOBPIN,OBPIO, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{O} } \right) \le d\left( {OBPI_{N} ,OBPI_{O} } \right);$$\end{document}dOBPIM,OBPIO≤dOBPIN,OBPIO;

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( {d_{5} } \right)\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,If\,\,OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} \,{\text{and}}\,OBPI_{O} \,$$\end{document}d5IfOBPIM,OBPINandOBPIO be any three orthoparian belief-plausibility intervals, then, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{O} } \right) \le d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) + d\left( {OBPI_{N} ,OBPI_{O} } \right)$$\end{document}dOBPIM,OBPIO≤dOBPIM,OBPIN+dOBPIN,OBPIO.

To check the validity of proposed distance measure, we need to prove all the above axioms for orthoparian belief-plausibility intervals.

Theorem 2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z} = \left\{ {z_{1} ,z_{2} ,...,z_{n} } \right\}$$\end{document}Z~=z1,z2,...,zn be universe of discourses, then the proposed distance measure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{BPH} \left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right)$$\end{document}dBPHOBPIM,OBPIN for belief-plausibility intervals in q-ROFSs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N\,$$\end{document}N satisfy all axioms of Definition 9.

Axiom 1

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d\left( {OBPI_{M} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) \le 1$$\end{document}0≤dOBPIM,OBPIN≤1.

Proof

Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$OBPI_{M} \,{\text{and}}\,OBPI_{N}$$\end{document}OBPIMandOBPIN are two belief-plausibility intervals on q-ROFSs in the universe of discourse \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Z} = \left\{ {z_{1} ,z_{2} ,...,z_{n} } \right\}.$$\end{document}Z~=z1,z2,...,zn.
