Dendrogram for the hierarchical clustering.

The above example shows the applicability and validity of our proposed similarity measure Eq. (10) between two q-ROFSs in clustering. According to the different \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau$$\end{document}τ levels, we may use our similarity measure Eq. (10) between two q-ROFSs to construct a hierarchical clustering for the data in the form of q-ROFSs.

Comparison of proposed measures with existing ones

We compare our proposed measures with the recently suggested distance measure between q-ROFSs given by Wang et al.24 to show the superiority, suitability and reasonability of our proposed measures. The distance measure between q-ROFSs given by Wang et al.24 is as follows:13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \begin{gathered} D_{JS\_2D} \left( {Q_{1} ,Q_{2} } \right) = \left[ {\frac{1}{2}\left( {\chi_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right)Log\frac{{2\chi_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}}{{\chi_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right) + \chi_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}} + \chi_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)Log\frac{{2\chi_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}}{{\chi_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right) + \chi_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}}} \right.} \right. \hfill \\ \left. {\left. {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \vartheta_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right)Log\frac{{2\vartheta_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}}{{\vartheta_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right) + \vartheta_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}} + \vartheta_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)Log\frac{{2\vartheta_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}}{{\vartheta_{{Q_{1} }}^{q} \left( x \right) + \vartheta_{{Q_{2} }}^{q} \left( x \right)}}} \right)} \right]^{\frac{1}{2}} \hfill \\ \end{gathered} $$\end{document}DJS_2DQ1,Q2=12χQ1qxLog2χQ1qxχQ1qx+χQ2qx+χQ2qxLog2χQ2qxχQ1qx+χQ2qx+ϑQ1qxLog2ϑQ1qxϑQ1qx+ϑQ2qx+ϑQ2qxLog2ϑQ2qxϑQ1qx+ϑQ2qx12

For comparison, we extract similarity measure from the above distance by using the relation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\text{similarity}}\,{ = 1 - }\,{\text{distance}}$$\end{document}similarity=1-distance as:14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ S\left( {Q_{1} ,Q_{2} } \right) = 1 - D_{JS\_2D} \left( {Q_{1} ,Q_{2} } \right) $$\end{document}SQ1,Q2=1-DJS_2DQ1,Q2

We use the example 3 of manuscript in which three different patterns are given which are expressed by three q-ROFSs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{1}$$\end{document}M1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{2}$$\end{document}M2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{3}$$\end{document}M3 respectively, and can be represented as:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{1} = \left\{ {\left\langle {z_{1} ,0.4,0.8} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{2} ,0.5,0.7} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{3} ,0.6,0.5} \right\rangle } \right\}$$\end{document}M1=z1,0.4,0.8,z2,0.5,0.7,z3,0.6,0.5, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{2} = \left\{ {\left\langle {z_{1} ,0.2,0.9} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{2} ,0.4,0.7} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{3} ,0.6,0.7} \right\rangle } \right\}$$\end{document}M2=z1,0.2,0.9,z2,0.4,0.7,z3,0.6,0.7 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{3} = \left\{ {\left\langle {z_{1} ,0.7,0.6} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{2} ,0.9,0.8} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{3} ,0.8,0.6} \right\rangle } \right\}$$\end{document}M3=z1,0.7,0.6,z2,0.9,0.8,z3,0.8,0.6 on the universe of discourse \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z = \left\{ {z_{1} ,z_{2} ,z_{3} } \right\}$$\end{document}Z=z1,z2,z3.

Another pattern \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = \left\{ {\left\langle {z_{1} ,0.5,0.7} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{2} ,0.5,0.6} \right\rangle ,\left\langle {z_{3} ,0.7,0.5} \right\rangle } \right\}$$\end{document}N=z1,0.5,0.7,z2,0.5,0.6,z3,0.7,0.5 is given as a sample. Our aim is to identify the pattern to which the sample \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N$$\end{document}N resembles. The results of the proposed similarity measures Eqs. (8)–(10) are:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{L} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{1} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.859,$$\end{document}SLOBPIM1,OBPIN=0.859, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{L} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{2} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.756,$$\end{document}SLOBPIM2,OBPIN=0.756, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{L} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{3} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.670,$$\end{document}SLOBPIM3,OBPIN=0.670,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{Q} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{1} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.753,$$\end{document}SQOBPIM1,OBPIN=0.753, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{Q} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{2} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.608,$$\end{document}SQOBPIM2,OBPIN=0.608, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{Q} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{3} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.401,$$\end{document}SQOBPIM3,OBPIN=0.401,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{E} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{1} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.792,$$\end{document}SEOBPIM1,OBPIN=0.792, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{E} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{2} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.657,$$\end{document}SEOBPIM2,OBPIN=0.657, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{E} \left( {OBPI_{{M_{3} }} ,OBPI_{N} } \right) = 0.555.$$\end{document}SEOBPIM3,OBPIN=0.555.

The results obtained from Eq. (14) are:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S\left( {M_{1} ,N} \right) = 0.9364$$\end{document}SM1,N=0.9364, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S\left( {M_{2} ,N} \right) = 0.8724$$\end{document}SM2,N=0.8724, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S\left( {M_{3} ,N} \right) = 0.8310$$\end{document}SM3,N=0.8310.

The above results show that the given sample \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N$$\end{document}N resembles with the pattern \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{1}$$\end{document}M1 which confirms the correctness of our proposed measures. However, the existing distance measure of Eq. (13) has some draw backs. For example, if we consider degenerated case of IFS \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{i} = \left\{ {\left\langle {z,1,0} \right\rangle } \right\}$$\end{document}Mi=z,1,0 or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{i} = \left\{ {\left\langle {z,0,1} \right\rangle } \right\}$$\end{document}Mi=z,0,1 then, the existing distance measure of Eq. (13) become undefined. In contrast, our proposed measures handle this situation amicably. This effectively demonstrates the superiority of our proposed divergence measure over the existing one.

Construction of Orthopairian belief and plausible GRA

Making decisions is a regular activity that we all engage in on a daily basis. When using multiple criteria, we choose the best option by ranking all of the available options. To achieve this, it is important to ensure a better decision-making process.

GRA was first developed by Deng55 to handle imprecise and incomplete information in MCDM in grey setting. Literature shows that GRA has been employed by many researchers in q-ROFSs environment56,57 to find the best option from the given alternatives on the basis of respective Relative Grey Relational Degree (RGRD). In this section, we discuss our work on distance measure between belief and plausibility of q-ROFS which helps us provide a good way to tackle the MCCDM. We extended GRA technique to develop belief and plausible GRA with belief-plausible intervals of q-ROFS to solve multicriteria decision making issues.

We proposed a novel technique to tackle MCCDM issues with unidentified weights. The suggested distance measure among OBPIs is used to measure the distance between the alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{i} \,$$\end{document}Ai from belief-plausibility negative ideal solutions (BP-NIS) and belief-plausibility positive ideal solutions (BP-PIS) respectively and then utilization of GRA process for ranking of alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{i} \,\left\{ {i = 1,2,...,m} \right\}$$\end{document}Aii=1,2,...,m and criterias \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_{j} \left\{ {j = 1,2,...,n} \right\}$$\end{document}Cjj=1,2,...,n. The purpose of this problem is to select the best alternative among all existing alternatives. The step-wise algorithm of belief-Plausibility GRA is under.

Step 1: Construction of belief-plausibility decision matrix.

Let us consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A = \left\{ {A_{1} ,A_{2} ,...,A_{m} } \right\}$$\end{document}A=A1,A2,...,Am be a set of alternatives to criteria \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C = \left\{ {C_{1} ,C_{2} ,...,C_{n} } \right\}$$\end{document}C=C1,C2,...,Cn. The decision making matrix for q-ROFSs be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{D} = \left[ {\tilde{b}_{ij} } \right]_{m \times n} = \left[ {Bl_{ij} ,\,Pl_{ij} } \right]$$\end{document}D~=b~ijm×n=Blij,Plij, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{ij} = \mu_{ij}^{q}$$\end{document}Blij=μijq and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{ij} = 1 - \nu_{ij}^{q} .$$\end{document}Plij=1-νijq. The value of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Bl_{ij} = \mu_{ij}^{q}$$\end{document}Blij=μijq indicates the degree of belief and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Pl_{ij} = 1 - \nu_{ij}^{q}$$\end{document}Plij=1-νijq indicates the plausible degree against the alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{i}$$\end{document}Ai to criteria \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_{j}$$\end{document}Cj in such a way that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Bl_{ij} \le 1\,\,\,,\,\,\,0 \le Pl_{ij} \le 1\,$$\end{document}0≤Blij≤1,0≤Plij≤1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le Bl_{ij} \le Pl_{ij} \le 1$$\end{document}0≤Blij≤Plij≤1 remains unchanged. The decision matrix will be established as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \tilde{D} = \left[ {\widehat{{h_{ij} }}} \right]_{m \times n} = \left[ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {\left[ {Bl_{11} ,Pl_{11} } \right]} & {\left[ {Bl_{12} ,Pl_{12} } \right]} & . & . & . & {\left[ {Bl_{1n} ,Pl_{1n} } \right]} \\ {\left[ {Bl_{21} ,Pl_{21} } \right]} & {\left[ {Bl_{22} ,Pl_{22} } \right]} & . & . & . & {\left[ {Bl_{2n} ,Pl_{2n} } \right]} \\ . & . & . & . & . & . \\ . & . & . & . & . & . \\ . & . & . & . & . & . \\ {\left[ {Bl_{m1} ,Pl_{m1} } \right]} & {\left[ {Bl_{m2} ,Pl_{m2} } \right]} & . & . & . & {\left[ {Bl_{mn} ,Pl_{mn} } \right]} \\ \end{array} } \right] $$\end{document}D~=hij^m×n=Bl11,Pl11Bl12,Pl12...Bl1n,Pl1nBl21,Pl21Bl22,Pl22...Bl2n,Pl2n..................Blm1,Plm1Blm2,Plm2...Blmn,Plmn

Step 2: Normalize the decision matrix.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \tilde{D}_{ } = \left\{ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {I_{ij} = \left( {\mu_{ij} , v_{ij} } \right), } & {if C_{j} \in Cb (benefit \,\,criteria)} \\ {( I_{ij} )^{c} = \left( {v_{ij} , \mu_{ij} } \right),} & {if C_{j} \in Cc ({\text{Cos}} t \,\,criteria)} \\ \end{array} } \right. $$\end{document}D~=Iij=μij,vij,ifCj∈Cb(benefitcriteria)(Iij)c=vij,μij,ifCj∈Cc(Costcriteria)

Step 3: Determination of the weight of criterion.

Here, we find the weights of criterion. To find the weight criterias there are many ways. Let us consider that the weights of criterias \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_{j} \left\{ {j = 1,2,...,n} \right\}\,$$\end{document}Cjj=1,2,...,n are \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega_{j} ,\,j = 1,2,...,n$$\end{document}ωj,j=1,2,...,n with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{n} {\omega_{j} = 1}$$\end{document}∑j=1nωj=1 and also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\,0 \le \omega_{j} \le 1$$\end{document}0≤ωj≤1. Here weights are not given, so we suggest a new way to find weights as: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega_{j} = \frac{{\left( {{\raise0.7ex\hbox{${\left( {3Bl_{j} + Pl_{j} } \right)}$} \!\mathord{\left/ {\vphantom {{\left( {3Bl_{j} + Pl_{j} } \right)} 2}}\right.\kern-0pt} \!\lower0.7ex\hbox{$2$}}} \right)}}{{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n} {\left( {{\raise0.7ex\hbox{${\left( {3Bl_{j} + Pl_{j} } \right)}$} \!\mathord{\left/ {\vphantom {{\left( {3Bl_{j} + Pl_{j} } \right)} 2}}\right.\kern-0pt} \!\lower0.7ex\hbox{$2$}}} \right)} }}$$\end{document}ωj=3Blj+Plj/2∑i=1n3Blj+Plj/2.

Step 4: Positive and negative ideal solutions.

In this step we divide the criteria into two parts: one is for beneficial criteria and the other is for cost criteria denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_{1}$$\end{document}B1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_{2}$$\end{document}B2 respectively, which satisfying the property \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_{1} \subseteq C\,\,\,{\text{and}}\,\,\,B_{2} \subseteq C$$\end{document}B1⊆CandB2⊆C and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_{1} \cap B_{2} = \phi .$$\end{document}B1∩B2=ϕ. By the principle of GRA in belief and plausible q-ROF positive ideal solution (BP-PIS) and belief and plausible negative ideal solution (BP-NIS) is given below:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BP - PIS \, = \, I^{ + } = \left( {Bl_{ij}^{ + } , \, Pl_{ij}^{ + } } \right) \, = \left( {\mathop {\max }\limits_{i} Bl_{ij} ,\mathop {\min }\limits_{i} Pl_{ij} } \right)$$\end{document}BP-PIS=I+=Blij+,Plij+=maxiBlij,miniPlij for higher the best and

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BP - NIS \, = \, I^{ - } = \left( {Bl_{ij}^{ - } , \, Pl_{ij}^{ - } } \right) \, = \left( {\mathop {\min }\limits_{i} Bl_{ij} ,\mathop {\max }\limits_{i} Pl_{ij} } \right)$$\end{document}BP-NIS=I-=Blij-,Plij-=miniBlij,maxiPlij for lower the best.

Step 5: Calculate the q-ROF distance matrices:(i)Calculate the q-ROF-distance by using proposed distance between (BP-PIS) and q-ROF value of each alternative: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{ + } = \, \left[ {D\left( {I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } } \right)} \right]_{m \, \times \, n}$$\end{document}D+=DIij,Ij+m×n(ii)Evaluate the q-ROF-distance between (BP-NIS) and q-ROF value of each alternative: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{ - } = \, \left[ {D\left( {I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } } \right)} \right]_{m \, \times \, n}$$\end{document}D-=DIij,Ij-m×n

(i)Calculate the q-ROF-distance by using proposed distance between (BP-PIS) and q-ROF value of each alternative: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{ + } = \, \left[ {D\left( {I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } } \right)} \right]_{m \, \times \, n}$$\end{document}D+=DIij,Ij+m×n

Calculate the q-ROF-distance by using proposed distance between (BP-PIS) and q-ROF value of each alternative: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{ + } = \, \left[ {D\left( {I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } } \right)} \right]_{m \, \times \, n}$$\end{document}D+=DIij,Ij+m×n

(ii)Evaluate the q-ROF-distance between (BP-NIS) and q-ROF value of each alternative: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{ - } = \, \left[ {D\left( {I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } } \right)} \right]_{m \, \times \, n}$$\end{document}D-=DIij,Ij-m×n

Evaluate the q-ROF-distance between (BP-NIS) and q-ROF value of each alternative: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{ - } = \, \left[ {D\left( {I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } } \right)} \right]_{m \, \times \, n}$$\end{document}D-=DIij,Ij-m×n

Step 6: Find the grey relational coefficient (GRC) Matrix:

(a) Calculate the GRC of each alternative from BP-PIS:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\upxi }_{ij}^{ + } = \,\,\frac{{\mathop {\min }\limits_{1\, \le \,\,i\,\, \le \,m} \,\,\mathop {\min }\limits_{1\, \le \,j\, \le \,\,n} \,\,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } )\, + \,\rho .\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\, \le \,\,i\,\, \le \,m} \,\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\, \le \,j\, \le \,n} \,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } )}}{{\,\,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } )\, + \,\rho .\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\,\, \le \,\,i\,\, \le \,\,m} \,\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\,\, \le j\, \le \,n} \,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ + } )}}. $$\end{document}ξij+=min1≤i≤mmin1≤j≤nD(Iij,Ij+)+ρ.max1≤i≤mmax1≤j≤nD(Iij,Ij+)D(Iij,Ij+)+ρ.max1≤i≤mmax1≤j≤nD(Iij,Ij+).(b) Calculate the GRC of each alternative from BP-NIS:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\upxi }_{ij}^{ - } = \,\,\frac{{\mathop {\min }\limits_{1\,\, \le \,\,i\,\, \le \,\,m} \,\,\mathop {\min }\limits_{1\,\, \le j\,\, \le \,\,n} \,\,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } )\, + \,\rho .\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\,\, \le \,\,i\,\, \le \,\,m} \,\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\, \le \,\,j\,\, \le \,\,n} \,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } )}}{{\,\,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } )\, + \,\rho .\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\,\, \le \,i\, \le \,m} \,\,\mathop {\max }\limits_{1\,\, \le \,\,j\,\, \le \,\,n} \,D(I_{ij} ,\,I_{j}^{ - } )}}. $$\end{document}ξij-=min1≤i≤mmin1≤j≤nD(Iij,Ij-)+ρ.max1≤i≤mmax1≤j≤nD(Iij,Ij-)D(Iij,Ij-)+ρ.max1≤i≤mmax1≤j≤nD(Iij,Ij-).where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho $$\end{document}ρ is identification coefficient and taken \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho $$\end{document}ρ =0.5 for stabilization.

Step 7: Evaluate weighted grey relational degrees (GRD):

We use the above equations of (a) and (b) to find weighted grey relational degrees \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\upxi }_{j}^{ + }$$\end{document}ξj+ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\upxi }_{j}^{ - }$$\end{document}ξj-, respectively.

(i) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi_{j}^{ + } = \sum\limits_{j = 1}^{n} {\omega_{j} . I_{ij}^{ + } }$$\end{document}ξj+=∑j=1nωj.Iij+ (ii) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi_{j}^{ - } = \sum\limits_{j = 1}^{n} {\omega_{j} . I_{ij}^{ - } }$$\end{document}ξj-=∑j=1nωj.Iij-.

Step 8: Find relative grey relational degree RGRD for each alternative:

Calculate relative grey relational degree using the below formula:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \,\xi_{i} = \frac{{\xi_{j}^{ + } }}{{\xi_{j}^{ + } + \xi_{j}^{ - } }} $$\end{document}ξi=ξj+ξj++ξj-

Step 9: Ranking:

Finally, the alternatives are ranked on the basis of their increasing RGRD values.

The flow chart of our proposed OBP-GRA algorithm is shown in Fig. 4 below.Figure 4Flow chart for the new modified OBP-GRA algorithm.

Flow chart for the new modified OBP-GRA algorithm.

Now, the above algorithm is used to tackle a daily life complex problem.

Example 4

COVID-19 sickness caused by coronavirus SARS-CoV-2 is the most alarming and disastrous issue of almost all over the world since 11th March 2020 which is rapidly increasing day by day and is declared as pandemic by World Health Organization (WHO)58. More than 72 countries are affected with this deadly virus with almost 772 million confirmed cases and more than 6 million death cases so far which is updated on 19th November, 202359. According to the Covid-19 epidemiological update of 19th January, 2024, during the 28-day period from December 11, 2023, to January 7, 2024, nearly 1.1 million new cases were reported worldwide, a 4% increase over the previous 28-day period. With 8700 new deaths recorded, there were 26% fewer new deaths than there were during the preceding 28 days. As of January 7, 2024, there had been over seven million recorded deaths and over 774 million confirmed cases worldwide60,61.

Numerous COVID-19 vaccines have been approved for use by WHO. Early in December 2020, the first mass vaccination campaign was launched. There are several vaccines introduced to overcome the variants of this deadly pandemic. Some vaccines are too costly and not affordable for under-developed countries, and some are easily available. Some of the vaccines are rapidly effective but have number of side effects. So it is much more difficult for a common man affected by COVID-19 to choose the most effective vaccine among all. To check the effectiveness of these vaccines, assume that there are four possible alternatives: (a) Vaccine \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{1}$$\end{document}A1, (b) Vaccine \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{2}$$\end{document}A2, (c) Vaccine \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{3}$$\end{document}A3 and (d) Vaccine \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{4}$$\end{document}A4. Based upon the viewpoint of the relevant medical experts, the following four important criterias are set to assess these four vaccines. Conclusions

The belief and plausible functions in the framework of q-ROFSs allows for a more dynamic and context aware representation of vague, uncertain and incomplete information. Since, the literature contains no generalization of ET to q-ROFSs. Therefore, we have expressed belief in term of membership and plausibility in term of 1-nonmembership functions of q-ROFSs respectively which leads to generalization of ET to q-ROFSs. Then, the OBPIs based on suggested belief and plausible measures of q-ROFSs are constructed. Based on the proposed interpretation together with exploitation of notion of Hausdorff distance, we have developed a novel way to calculate distance and similarity measures between q-ROFSs in the context of ET. Finally, proposed distance is utilized to develop OBP-GRA for the selection of COVID-19 vaccines. Relevant numerical simulations and applications to resolve complex daily life issues attest the reasonability, validity, practicality and suitability of interpretation of q-ROFSs in the frame work of ET.

Future research directions

In the future, we will further investigate the possibility to extend our research work to hesitant fuzzy sets, Bipolar fuzzy sets, Neutrosophic fuzzy sets and aggregation operators with application to MCDM, pattern recognition, clustering, image processing etc.
